INTRODUCTION



This manual presents all the operations carried out in a workshop after the disassembly of the passen-

ger seats of the EMB-120 "BRASILIA" aircraft. It allows a technician not acquainted with the equipment

to restore it to its operational conditions.



This manual contains the component description and operation, detailed instructions for disassembling,

checking, repairing and reassembling the component, and an i

DESCRIPTION AND OPERATION

1. General

The passenger seats are arranged into two rows in the aircraft, one comprising single seats and

another comprising double seats. In some configurations, the last row may also comprise triple

seats.

2. Description

A. The Single Passenger Seat (Figure 1) configurations listed below may be found on the "EMB-

120" Brasilia:

(1) First row single passenger seat with plug-in type snack table.

(2)

G. The backrest structure consists of a rigid composite structure attached to the seat structure and

molded foam cushion is glued to this structure, then covered with fitted upholstery, which, in turn,

is secured with Velcro fastener.



H. The seat bottom cushion is made of molded foam and is covered by the upholstery which is

secured with Velcro fastener.

CHECK

1. General

A. The following checks must be carried out on the passenger seats:

(1) Visually check all bolts, screws, and nuts removed for damaged threads and/or heads.

(2) Visually check the bushings removed for chafing.

(3) Visually check the parts for nicks, cracks, cuts, corrosion, scoring, excessive wear, and

defects that could impair an efficient seat operation.

B. Determine Damage (figure 602).

(a) Remove the damaged plies from laminates and sandwich structure areas using No. 80

sandpaper, fine abrasive, or trim out the damaged surface, keeping a geometric shape

(oval, circular or rectangular), exercising care not to damage the honeycomb or the sur-

face around the damaged area.



(b) Mark the area to receive the repair layers with adhesive tape, leaving 1-in (25-mm) mar-



gin for the additional layer to settle.



(c)

(6) Apply vacuum of 22.0 in.Hg (0.745 bars).



(7) Allow the repair to cure at the room temperature, or accelerate the cure by applying heat until

the temperature reaches 176°F (80°C).



(8) Remove the vacuum bag and lightly sand the edges and corners to produce a smooth finish

without damaging the fibers.
